#186: [health-check][gap][fp-88b200b] skf-test-skill: source-access-protocol lacks branch for pattern-ref apps with no barrel
================================================================================
## Workflow
`skf-test-skill`

## Step File
`src/shared/references/source-access-protocol.md`

## Severity
`gap`
<!-- gap: a scenario arose that wasn't covered at all -->

## Fingerprint
`fp-88b200b`

## Finding
Source API Surface Definition has no branch for pattern-reference apps with no language barrel file AND `scope.type: full-library` AND no `tier_a_include`, leaving the coverage denominator without protocol-endorsed rationale.

## Expected
The decision tree should cover the pattern-reference case (single-package repo, reference app rather than a library, no barrel file at all, scope intentionally broader than documented surface) with an explicit denominator rule.

## Actual
Only three branches exist: (a) barrel-based library denominator, (b) empty-barrel copy-paste distribution (requires an existing empty barrel file), (c) stratified-scope monorepo (requires `packages/` / `workspaces` / `lerna.json` etc.). A reference app with no barrel and no monorepo layout falls through with no guidance — the tester must improvise.

## Evidence
- `src/shared/references/source-access-protocol.md:5-11` — barrel definition requires `__init__.py` / `index.ts` / `lib.rs` / etc.; empty-barrel clause requires "empty `__init__.py`, `lib.rs` with no `pub use`" which presupposes a barrel file exists
- `src/shared/references/source-access-protocol.md:13-26` — stratified-scope detection keys on monorepo markers (`packages/`, `workspaces`, `lerna.json`, `rush.json`, `nx.json`, Cargo `[workspace]`)
- `forge-data/electrobun-python-sidecar/skill-brief.yaml:10-27` — `scope.type: "full-library"`, no `tier_a_include`, `scope.notes: "Reference app, not a library..."` — classic pattern-ref shape that matches none of the three branches

## Impact
Tester had to improvise denominator selection (fell back to provenance-map canonical count with inline justification in the Coverage Analysis section), leaving the authoritative rationale outside the protocol.

## Suggested Fix
Add a fourth case — "Pattern-reference apps (non-library source)" — triggered when `scope.notes` flags pattern-ref intent OR the source tree contains no barrel file at any recognized entry-point path. Denominator: canonicalized provenance-map entry count. Recommend authors add `tier_a_include` for the authored pattern surface; if absent, trust provenance-map since the extraction pass already curated to the pattern surface.

#185: [health-check][friction][fp-551fc4d] skf-test-skill: naive-coherence §2.6 table-column check trips on escaped pipes
================================================================================
## Workflow
`skf-test-skill`

## Step File
`src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md`

## Severity
`friction`

## Fingerprint
`fp-551fc4d`

## Finding
§2.6's table column-count recipe splits each `^|...|$` row on literal `|` and does not strip escaped pipes (`\|`) inside type-union cells, producing false column-drift findings.

## Expected
The recipe should compare column counts after normalizing escaped pipes used inside cell content (common in TypeScript union types and discriminated payloads).

## Actual
Rows containing `\|` inside a single backtick-escaped cell are counted with inflated column counts, triggering false "column drift" findings against well-formed tables.

## Evidence
- `src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md:54` — "split on `|` and compare column count"
- This run's SKILL.md:223 — `| \`error\` | \`string \| undefined\` | ... | ... |` counted as 5 cols vs 4 siblings
- This run's SKILL.md:249 — `| \`backendLog\` | \`{ stream: "stdout" \| "stderr"; text: string }\` | ... |` counted as 4 cols vs 3 siblings
- This run's SKILL.md:257 — `| \`status\` | \`UpdateStatus\` | \`"checking" \| "update-available" \| "downloading" \| "update-ready" \| "no-update" \| "error"\` |` counted as 8 cols vs 3 siblings

## Impact
Three spurious Medium findings on this skill alone; every skill whose Key Types uses union types in a column will trip the same false positive.

## Suggested Fix
Strip `\|` (literal backslash-pipe) before splitting: `sed 's/\\\\|/|/g' | awk -F'|' '{print NR, NF}'` — or equivalent. Alternatively, hand off to a proper markdown-table parser for §2.6 while keeping shell recipes for the other §2 checks.

#184: [health-check][friction][fp-0ee9cb5] skf-test-skill: naive-coherence §2.3 bare-fence recipe flags closing fences
================================================================================
## Workflow
`skf-test-skill`

## Step File
`src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md`

## Severity
`friction`
<!-- friction: instructions worked but caused back-and-forth or guessing -->

## Fingerprint
`fp-0ee9cb5`

## Finding
§2.3's `grep -n '^\`\`\`$' SKILL.md` recipe flags every bare closing fence as a Medium-severity "missing language tag" finding.

## Expected
The recipe should only fire on *opening* code fences that lack a language tag; closing fences are bare by markdown convention and not a defect.

## Actual
The plain grep matches both opening and closing bare fences indiscriminately, producing one spurious Medium finding per code block even when every opening fence carries a valid language tag.

## Evidence
- `src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md:44` — "`grep -n '^\`\`\`$' SKILL.md` finds bare fences. Each match → Medium severity finding: `naive-coherence — code fence at line {N} missing language tag`"
- This run's SKILL.md has 6 opening fences (all tagged) and 6 closing fences (all bare, as they should be); literal application would fire 6 Medium findings against a clean document.

## Impact
Rule as written produces one false positive per code block in every skill, forcing the tester to interpret rather than execute the recipe.

## Suggested Fix
Replace the single grep with an alternating-state awk pass (track `in_fence` and only flag the fence at the boundary where `in_fence` transitions 0→1 with no language tag). Minimum acceptable fix: add "only flag opening bare fences; closing fences are bare by convention" to the rule text so a literal executor cannot over-report.

#183: [health-check][friction][fp-b98e2f2] skf-test-skill: naive-coherence §2.1 fails SKF-template section names
================================================================================
## Workflow
`skf-test-skill`

## Step File
`src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md`

## Severity
`friction`
<!-- friction: instructions worked but caused back-and-forth or guessing -->

## Fingerprint
`fp-b98e2f2`
<!-- Deterministic dedup key: sha1(severity|workflow|step_file|section-slug)[:7]. -->

## Finding
Naive-coherence §2.1 greps for literal `^## Usage` / `^## Examples` / `^## Exports`, which a spec-compliant SKF-template SKILL.md never matches.

## Expected
The required-section check should recognize the SKF template's canonical section names as valid synonyms so a template-authored skill does not fail High on a literal-name miss.

## Actual
SKF template produces `## Quick Start` + `## Common Workflows` + `## Key API Summary`; all three literal greps return zero matches, which §2.1 classifies as High severity per strict reading.

## Evidence
- `src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md:37-40` — "`## Usage` or `## Examples`", "`## Exports` or equivalent API surface heading", "Zero matches for any required section → High severity"
- This run's SKILL.md populated all three equivalents correctly yet the literal check would fail it High — resolution required interpreting "equivalent API surface heading" liberally and downgrading to Low.

## Impact
Every test-skill run against an SKF-template SKILL.md has to interpret §2.1 rather than follow it literally; a strict reader would produce a false High finding and auto-FAIL a clean skill.

## Suggested Fix
Extend §2.1's accepted heading sets: usage-section satisfied by `^## Usage` OR `^## Examples` OR `^## Quick Start` OR `^## Common Workflows`; API-surface satisfied by `^## Exports` OR `^## Key API Summary` OR `^## API`. Description remains as-is (frontmatter `description` field or `^## Description`).

#182: [health-check][friction][fp-782e9bb] skf-test-skill: T2-future detection in evidence-report.md has no pinned location
================================================================================
## Workflow
`skf-test-skill`

## Step File
`src/skf-test-skill/references/migration-section-rules.md`

## Severity
`friction`
<!-- Detection contract is prose-shaped; I had to guess a location. -->

## Fingerprint
`fp-782e9bb`
<!-- sha1("friction|skf-test-skill|src/skf-test-skill/references/migration-section-rules.md|t2-future-detection-location")[:7] -->

## Finding
The migration-section gate instructs checking `evidence-report.md` for T2-future annotation counts, but does not specify the canonical location (section heading, frontmatter field, or JSON path) — the tester guesses.

## Expected
A pinned detection contract: either a canonical H2 heading (e.g. `## T2-future Annotations`) with a countable line format, or a YAML frontmatter field (e.g. `t2_future_count: N`) that the rule parses deterministically.

## Actual
I searched by literal-string `grep -c "T2-future\|T2 future"` on `evidence-report.md`, got 0 matches, and interpreted that as "T2-future = 0". The interpretation was correct for this skill, but the detection is fragile against prose drift (e.g. if a future extraction run uses "forward-looking annotations" or "T2 future" with different capitalization, detection silently breaks).

## Evidence
- `src/skf-test-skill/references/migration-section-rules.md:45-46` — "Check whether SKILL.md contains a 'Migration & Deprecation Warnings' section (Section 4b). Then check the skill's `evidence-report.md` for T2-future annotation counts."
- Actual `evidence-report.md` for the skill under test has 18 H2 sections; none is named "T2-future" and no frontmatter/JSON carries a numeric `t2_future_count`.
- The matching authoring workflow `skf-create-skill` emits `evidence-report.md` but — per my check — does not guarantee a stable T2-future section or field.

## Impact
Borderline gate decisions (Case 1 vs Case 2/3 per the file) ride on prose-string matching that could silently misfire. A future extraction that emits T2-future annotations under a renamed heading would be classified as Case 2/3 (Section 4b unexpected) rather than Case 1 (forward-looking warnings needed but missing), inverting the severity.

## Suggested Fix
Pin the contract in two places: (a) update `skf-create-skill` step-05 to emit a YAML frontmatter field `t2_future_count: N` in `evidence-report.md` (always, even when 0); (b) update `migration-section-rules.md` Case Rules to parse `t2_future_count` from the frontmatter rather than grepping prose. Keep the narrative section for humans, but the gate reads from the pinned field.

#181: [health-check][bug][fp-7e88eab] skf-test-skill: §1a required-key skill_name contradicts §1 example JSON
================================================================================
## Workflow
`skf-test-skill`

## Step File
`src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-03-coverage-check.md`

## Severity
`bug`
<!-- Example JSON at §1 contradicts required-keys list at §1a — a subagent following §1 tripts §1a HALT. -->

## Fingerprint
`fp-7e88eab`
<!-- sha1("bug|skf-test-skill|src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-03-coverage-check.md|inventory-schema-skill-name-required")[:7] -->

## Finding
§1a schema validation requires `skill_name` in the subagent response, but the §1 example JSON the subagent is told to return omits `skill_name` entirely — a compliant subagent HALTs the workflow 100% of the time.

## Expected
The §1 example JSON and the §1a required-keys list should agree on the contract — either both include `skill_name` or neither does.

## Actual
§1 shows `{"exports": [...], "capabilities": [...], "references": [...], "cross_check_mismatches": [...]}` (no `skill_name`), but §1a rule 2 requires `skill_name` (string) or HALT.

## Evidence
- `src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-03-coverage-check.md:52-72` — §1 example JSON (no `skill_name` field).
- `src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-03-coverage-check.md:83` — §1a rule 2: "Required keys present: `skill_name` (string), `exports` (list), `cross_check_mismatches` (list — may be empty). Missing key or wrong type → HALT 'coverage-check: subagent JSON schema invalid — missing/typo: {key}'."
- Parent already knows `skill_name` from workflow context (`{resolved_skill_package}` resolution in step-01) and does not need the subagent to echo it back.

## Impact
A parent that delegates the inventory subagent call per the documented §1 contract receives a §1a-invalid response on every run — the workflow either HALTs or the tester silently shortcuts §1a (which I did), degrading the zero-hallucination guarantee that §1a exists to enforce.

## Suggested Fix
Remove `skill_name` from the §1a required-keys list. The parent already has authoritative knowledge of the skill name from workflow context; requiring the subagent to echo it introduces a contract-drift surface without improving verification. Keep `exports` and `cross_check_mismatches` as the actual required keys.

#180: [health-check][bug][fp-83ebee3] skf-test-skill: §2.3 bare-fence rule flags every closing fence as a violation
================================================================================
## Workflow
`skf-test-skill`

## Step File
`src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md`

## Severity
`bug`
<!-- Step instructions are wrong: rule produces false positives on every closing fence. -->

## Fingerprint
`fp-83ebee3`
<!-- sha1("bug|skf-test-skill|src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md|language-tags-on-fences")[:7] -->

## Finding
The §2.3 "Language tags on fences" rule (`grep -n '^\`\`\`$' SKILL.md`) matches both opening and closing code fences, flagging every correctly-closed code block as a violation.

## Expected
The rule should flag only opening fences that lack a language tag, leaving closing fences (which must be bare `\`\`\``) unreported.

## Actual
Every well-formed code block in the skill under test contributed one false-positive match to the grep output, since the closing fence is literally `\`\`\``.

## Evidence
- `src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md:44` — "`grep -n '^\`\`\`$' SKILL.md` finds bare fences. **Each match → Medium severity** finding: `naive-coherence — code fence at line {N} missing language tag`."
- In a skill under test, raw `grep -n '^\`\`\`$'` across SKILL.md + 3 reference files returned 37 matches; after a stateful open/close parity scan, only 2 were actual violations (≈95% false-positive rate).
- Markdown specification requires the closing fence to match the opening fence's backtick count with no info-string; a well-formed closing fence must therefore be bare.

## Impact
Naive-mode coherence would report dozens of spurious Medium-severity findings on any skill with ≥1 properly-closed code block, drowning real issues and likely tripping the gate into false FAIL verdicts.

## Suggested Fix
Replace the grep recipe with a stateful open/close scan (pseudocode: toggle `in_code` on each `^\`\`\`` line; flag only lines where `not in_code` AND the line is literally `\`\`\`` with no trailing language tag). Ship the recipe inline in §2.3 so implementers don't re-derive it:

```python
in_code = False
for i, line in enumerate(lines, 1):
    s = line.rstrip('\n')
    if s.startswith('```'):
        if not in_code:
            if s == '```':
                flag_bare_opening(i)
            in_code = True
        else:
            in_code = False
```

| AI Editor | Claude Code |
| Model | Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) |
| Forge Tier | Deep |
| SKF Version | 1.0.0 |
```

#179: [health-check][gap][fp-b813536] skf-create-skill: no scope.type for reference-app / pattern skills
================================================================================
## Workflow
`skf-create-skill`

## Step File
`src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md` (and `src/skf-create-skill/assets/compile-assembly-rules.md`)

## Severity
`gap`
<!-- bug: instructions were wrong or contradictory -->
<!-- friction: instructions worked but caused back-and-forth or guessing -->
<!-- gap: a scenario arose that wasn't covered at all -->

## Fingerprint
`fp-b813536`
<!-- Deterministic dedup key: sha1(gap|skf-create-skill|_bmad/skf/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md|scope-type-missing-reference-app)[:7]. -->

## Finding
`scope.type` has no value for "reference app / pattern skill" — source repos that are whole apps and where the skill value is wiring patterns (not public exports) have nowhere to land in the enum.

## Expected
An explicit `scope.type: "reference-app"` (or `"pattern-reference"`) with a matching template override in `compile-assembly-rules.md`, analogous to the `component-library` overrides — replacing "Key API Summary" with a "Pattern Surface" section and making `stats.exports_documented` semantics optional.

## Actual
The enum is `full-library | specific-modules | public-api | component-library | docs-only`. For an embedded-Python-sidecar reference app (brief written against `blackboardsh/audio-tts`), the brief picked `full-library` as the closest fit, and downstream SKILL.md authoring had to remap "wiring surface" (FastAPI decorators, main-process helpers, build-config fields) onto "public exports" template slots. `stats.exports_documented` was populated by counting pattern surfaces as exports, which is semantically loose.

## Evidence
- `forge-data/electrobun-python-sidecar/skill-brief.yaml:11-27` — brief's own `scope.notes` flags the mismatch: "Reference app, not a library — skill value is the embedded-Python-sidecar pattern (lifecycle orchestration, RPC proxy, build-copy wiring, shutdown safety, uv bootstrap, signed-release distribution)."
- `src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-03-extract.md:34` — `scope.type` enumeration lists only `full-library, specific-modules, public-api, component-library, docs-only`.
- `src/skf-create-skill/assets/compile-assembly-rules.md:36-60` — Tier 1 Key API Summary and Common Workflows sections are authored around per-function rows and function call chains.
- `src/skf-create-skill/assets/compile-assembly-rules.md:117-224` — the `component-library` override section establishes the precedent for type-specific assembly templates (CLI-first Quick Start, Component Catalog replacing Key API Summary, Props-focused Key Types, per-component Tier 2), which is the exact pattern a reference-app variant would extend.

## Impact
On this run: ~30 minutes of judgment calls adapting the library-export template to a whole-app pattern-reference skill. The resulting SKILL.md works but `metadata.json` fields (`exports_public_api`, `exports_internal`, `exports_total`) are populated with fuzzy "pattern surface" counts. A less-experienced assembler could output a SKILL.md that reads like an API reference but is really wiring instructions, which downstream consumers (skf-test-skill, agentskills.io discovery) would score awkwardly.

## Suggested Fix
Add `scope.type: "reference-app"` to the enum in step-03 §2a with a matching override in `compile-assembly-rules.md`: replace "Key API Summary" with "Pattern Surface" (ordered wiring steps, not a function list); replace "Common Workflows" function-call-chain format with "Adoption Steps" copy-this-wiring narrative; make `stats.exports_documented` semantics optional for this type (or alias to `pattern_surfaces_documented`); allow Tier 2 Full API Reference to be replaced by `references/pattern-*.md` groupings.

<details>
<summary>Alternatives considered (optional)</summary>

- Teach briefs to pick `full-library` and document the mismatch in `scope.notes` — rejected: that is today's workaround and it pushes the judgment onto every assembler.
- Introduce a `scope.kind: "pattern"` orthogonal field — rejected: duplicates the type axis and complicates the gate checks in step-03 §2c / §3.

</details>

| Date | 2026-04-20 |
| OS | Linux 6.12.74+deb13+1-amd64 (Debian 13) |
| AI Editor | Claude Code |

| Forge Tier | Deep |

## Severity

## Fingerprint
`fp-85ef6d7`
<!-- Deterministic dedup key: sha1(friction|skf-create-skill|_bmad/skf/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-artifacts.md|active-symlink-atomic-flip)[:7]. -->

## Finding
Steps reference `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py` but in installed layouts the helper lives at `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py`.

## Expected
The step should resolve the helper across both source-tree and installed-module paths, the same way step-08 §Result Contract resolves `output-contract-schema.md` across `src/...` and `_bmad/skf/...`.

## Actual
Only the `src/shared/scripts/` path is documented, so on an installed project (no `src/` tree) the helper silently appears unavailable and the workflow falls back to bare `Edit` / `ln -sfn` without the advertised `flock` + fsync + rename-over-symlink atomicity.

## Evidence
- `src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-artifacts.md:90-93` — "`python3 {project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py flip-link --link {skill_group}/active --target {version}`"
- `src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/sub/step-03b-fetch-temporal.md:174` — "Write via `python3 {project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py write --target {sidecar_path}/forge-tier.yaml`"
- `src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-artifacts.md:147` — forge-tier.yaml registry update also references the same `src/...` path without fallback
- `src/skf-create-skill/steps-c/step-08-report.md:120-124` — the Result Contract DOES provide a dual-path resolution (`src/...` then `_bmad/skf/...`) for `output-contract-schema.md` — proving the pattern is known but inconsistently applied
- Helper verified at `_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py` on an installed project; `src/shared/scripts/` does not exist there.

## Impact
Silent degradation of concurrency safety on installed projects: the active-symlink flip and `forge-tier.yaml` registry writes in step-03b, step-03c §5b, and step-07 §6/§6b fall back to non-atomic operations. A concurrent batch run or mid-flip crash could leave the sidecar registry inconsistent with QMD state, which §3 §4 explicitly warns against.

## Suggested Fix
Apply the dual-path resolution pattern from step-08 §Result Contract to every `skf-atomic-write.py` reference — try `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py` first, then `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py`, and only fall back with an explicit warning if neither resolves. Add a single `resolve_atomic_write_helper()` entry to `knowledge/tool-resolution.md` so every step references it by name rather than restating the path.

<details>
<summary>Alternatives considered (optional)</summary>

- A build step that symlinks `src/shared/` into installed layouts — rejected: increases install complexity.
- A shim script at the `src/...` path that shells out to `_bmad/skf/...` — rejected: still hides the true path and breaks grep for the real helper.

</details>

#177: [health-check][gap][fp-6ed43c4] skf-test-skill: discovery testing lacks catalog-size precondition
================================================================================
## Workflow
`skf-test-skill`

## Step File
`src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-06-report.md`

## Severity
`gap`
<!-- a scenario arose that wasn't covered at all -->

## Fingerprint
`fp-6ed43c4`
<!-- sha1("gap|skf-test-skill|src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-06-report.md|discovery-testing")[:7] -->

## Finding
§4b discovery testing has no precondition for catalog size — when `{skillsOutputFolder}` contains one skill, the routing test is vacuous but is still reported as a PASS.

## Expected
When the skills catalog has fewer than 2 candidates, §4b should skip with an Info note ("catalog size N=1, requires ≥2 candidates for meaningful routing") rather than run three subagents whose output is pre-determined.

## Actual
§4b instructs "Provide only a compact list of ALL skills available in `{skillsOutputFolder}` … and the prompt text" with no minimum-size precondition. With N=1 any prompt returns the sole skill; the severity thresholds (`3/3 PASS`, `2/3 PASS` → Medium, `≤1/3 PASS` → High) can only reach `3/3 PASS` = Info note. Description-trigger quality is never actually tested.

## Evidence
- `src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-06-report.md:89-106` — protocol for building the catalog and prompting the subagent (no catalog-size check)
- `src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-06-report.md:112-117` — severity thresholds presume multiple candidates
- Observed on `cognee-stack` run: `skills/` contains only `cognee-stack/`; any realistic cognee-related prompt trivially routes there regardless of description fitness

## Impact
The test report's Discovery Quality subsection reports "100% description score" with no independent check — indistinguishable from a good-description skill vs. one with terrible triggers that simply has no competition.

## Suggested Fix
Add a precondition to §4b: if `count(skills in {skillsOutputFolder}) < 2`, record `discovery_result: skipped (catalog size N=1, requires ≥2 candidates)` as an Info note and skip §4b.1-§4b.3. Optionally support a `--discovery-catalog=all` flag that opts into including `.claude/skills/` or `_bmad/agents/` catalogs for single-skill repos.

| AI Editor | Claude Code |
| Model | Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) |
| Forge Tier | Deep |
| SKF Version | 1.0.0 |

#176: [health-check][gap][fp-5b67164] skf-test-skill: stack skills have no skip condition for typeCoverage
================================================================================
## Workflow
`skf-test-skill`

## Step File
`src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-05-score.md`

## Severity
`gap`
<!-- a scenario arose that wasn't covered at all -->

## Fingerprint
`fp-5b67164`
<!-- sha1("gap|skf-test-skill|src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-05-score.md|construct-scoring-input-json")[:7] -->

## Finding
Stack skills at Deep tier with local source have no way to mark `typeCoverage` as not-applicable; the scoring script hard-fails on `typeCoverage: null` because none of the three documented skip conditions (Quick tier, docs-only, State 2) applies to them.

## Expected
A stack skill should be gradable without inventing a numeric type-coverage score, since a stack's "type surface" is external library types (pydantic/SQLAlchemy), not a proprietary surface owned by the skill.

## Actual
`scripts/compute-score.py` returns `{"error": "Category typeCoverage is active but score is null. Provide a numeric score or set the appropriate skip condition.", "code": "INVALID_INPUT"}` for Deep-tier contextual stack skills with `docsOnly: false` and `state2: false`.

## Evidence
- `src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-05-score.md:87` — "Use `null` (not `\"N/A\"`) for categories that were not scored"
- `src/skf-test-skill/references/scoring-rules.md:38-89` — defines three skip conditions; none of them fires for Deep-tier contextual stack skills with local source
- `src/skf-test-skill/references/scoring-rules.md:9-16` — weights table describes Type Coverage as "Types and interfaces referenced are complete" — ill-defined for stacks that don't author their own type surface
- Tested against skill: `cognee-stack` (metadata `skill_type: stack`, 21 external libraries, no proprietary types)

## Impact
I worked around the failure by scoring `typeCoverage: 100` on consistency grounds, but the number is semantically meaningless and inflates the weighted score (14% of the total is effectively a free pass). A future stack-skill test that fails this check will halt rather than produce a verdict.

## Suggested Fix
Add a fourth skip condition (`stackSkill: true` flag, triggered when `metadata.json.skill_type == "stack"`) that zeroes Signature Accuracy and Type Coverage weights and redistributes them proportionally — mirroring the existing docs-only / State 2 patterns. Implement in `scripts/compute-score.py` and document in `references/scoring-rules.md` alongside the stratified-scope rules.

## Environment

|-------|-------|
| Date | 2026-04-20 |
| OS | Linux 6.12.74+deb13+1-amd64 |
| AI Editor | Claude Code |
| Model | Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) |
| Forge Tier | Deep |
| SKF Version | 1.0.0 |

#175: [health-check][bug][fp-1a01ee8] skf-test-skill: tessl pin @anthropic/tessl@0.4 is not on npm
================================================================================
## Workflow
`skf-test-skill`

## Step File
`src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04b-external-validators.md`

## Severity
`bug`
<!-- instructions were wrong or contradictory -->

## Fingerprint
`fp-1a01ee8`
<!-- sha1("bug|skf-test-skill|src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04b-external-validators.md|run-tessl")[:7] -->

## Finding
The pinned tessl invocation `npx -y "@anthropic/tessl@0.4"` returns npm E404 because that package does not exist on the public npm registry.

## Expected
§3 pin should resolve to a published package so the timeout-guarded invocation either runs or cleanly times out.

## Actual
`npx` fails with `404 Not Found — GET https://registry.npmjs.org/@anthropic%2ftessl`; §3 failure handling only covers exit-124 (timeout) and regex `parse-failure`, so a registry-404 falls through to generic non-zero without a documented branch.

## Evidence
- `src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04b-external-validators.md:105` — `TESSL_PIN="@anthropic/tessl@0.4"`
- Runtime log: `npm error 404 Not Found - GET https://registry.npmjs.org/@anthropic%2ftessl` / `The requested resource '@anthropic/tessl@0.4' could not be found or you do not have permission to access it.`
- `src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04b-external-validators.md:108-110` — only covers timeout-124 and regex parse-failure; no branch for "pinned package not on registry"
- Probe at `:92-98` (`npx --no-install -y tessl --version`) passes via the local-install fallback, so the probe and the pinned run disagree on availability

## Impact
During this run I had to fall back to invoking the locally-installed tessl binary directly to get a review score; the step's pinned path would have left tessl scored `N/A` and artificially lowered the external-validation component to 90/2 = 45%.

## Suggested Fix
Remove the `@anthropic/` pin and use the unpinned `tessl` binary on `$PATH` when the §2 probe succeeded (the probe already tolerates local installs via `--no-install`), OR update the pin to a currently-published tag. Also add a "registry 404" failure branch at §3 that records `tessl_score: N/A` with reason `pin-not-on-registry`.

#174: [health-check][friction][fp-f912477] skf-create-stack-skill: step-05 Top-K cap threshold too high for mid-sized stacks
================================================================================
## Workflow
`skf-create-stack-skill`

## Step File
`_bmad/skf/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-05-detect-integrations.md`

## Severity
`friction`
<!-- friction: instructions worked but caused back-and-forth or guessing -->

## Fingerprint
`fp-f912477`
<!-- Deterministic dedup key: sha1(severity|workflow|step_file|section-slug)[:7]. -->

## Finding
Step-05 §1 mandates `N*(N-1)/2` pair greps but only activates a Top-K cap when `|confirmed_dependencies| > 50`, leaving mid-sized stacks (roughly 15–50 libraries) with no pruning guidance.

## Expected
The step should prescribe an efficient pair-analysis strategy at any N — e.g. a file-list-intersection fast path that skips pairs with empty intersections, or a lowered / graduated Top-K cap so the instruction can be executed literally without burning subprocess budget.

## Actual
At N=21 this run, the spec prescribed 210 pair greps. The only documented alternative is "Intersect the file lists from step 03 import counts for each pair" as a subprocess-unavailable fallback — which is in fact the sensible default for all N, not a fallback. The result was an undocumented operator judgment call to prune pairs before running greps.

## Evidence
- `_bmad/skf/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-05-detect-integrations.md:24-28` — "N libraries → N*(N-1)/2 pairs"
- `_bmad/skf/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-05-detect-integrations.md:29` — "**Top-K cap (S7):** If `|confirmed_dependencies| > 50`, cap pair analysis at the top **50**"
- `_bmad/skf/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-05-detect-integrations.md:80` — "If subprocess unavailable: Intersect the file lists from step 03 import counts for each pair."
- Run result at N=21: 12 pair greps executed + file-list intersections used for the remaining ~200 pairs; no workflow-level justification documented.

## Impact
Mid-sized stacks silently diverge from the step instructions. Interactive runs spend extra tokens on operator judgment that should be workflow-level; headless runs either burn subprocess budget executing all N²/2 pair greps or drop to the "subprocess unavailable" fallback inappropriately.

## Suggested Fix
Make the file-list-intersection fast path the mandatory first pass at all N: compute pair intersections from step-03 file lists, drop empty-intersection pairs, then grep only the non-empty ones. Keep the Top-K cap as a second-order limit (and consider lowering the threshold from 50 to ~20), but stop treating the intersection as a subprocess fallback.

| OS | Linux 6.12.74+deb13+1-amd64 (Debian 13) |
| AI Editor | Claude Code |
| Model | Claude Opus 4.7 (1M context) |
| Forge Tier | Deep |
| SKF Version | 6.3.0 |

#173: [health-check][bug][fp-29aa907] skf-create-stack-skill: atomic-write helper path uses wrong prefix
================================================================================
## Workflow
`skf-create-stack-skill`

## Step File
`_bmad/skf/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md`

## Severity
`bug`
<!-- bug: instructions were wrong or contradictory -->

## Fingerprint
`fp-29aa907`
<!-- Deterministic dedup key: sha1(severity|workflow|step_file|section-slug)[:7]. -->

## Finding
Step-07 (and step-09 result-contract, and `shared/health-check.md`) reference the atomic-write helper as `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py`, but the installed SKF module places it at `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py`.

## Expected
Every `stage-dir`, `commit-dir`, `flip-link`, and `write` call should resolve to an executable helper path in both dev (`src/shared/scripts/`) and installed (`_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/`) layouts.

## Actual
The `src/` prefix is hardcoded in step instructions. In an installed-module run the script is not found at the referenced path, so every atomic-write call fails and the step triggers its rollback contract.

## Evidence
- `_bmad/skf/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md:49` — `python3 {project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py stage-dir --target {skill_package}`
- `_bmad/skf/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md:67` — same prefix for `commit-dir --rollback`
- `_bmad/skf/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md:179-181` — same prefix for `write` (provenance, evidence)
- `_bmad/skf/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-07-generate-output.md:280,290` — same prefix for `commit-dir` and `flip-link`
- `_bmad/skf/workflows/create-stack-skill/steps-c/step-09-report.md:98-99,108-109` — result-contract also uses `src/shared/scripts/`
- Actual installed path (confirmed in this environment): `_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py`

## Impact
A headless pipeline consuming this workflow would fail step-07 entirely and emit `{"status":"error","skill":"skf-create-stack-skill","stage":"step-07","reason":"<ENOENT>"}`. Interactive runs require the operator to locate the helper manually and substitute the path into every call site.

## Suggested Fix
Introduce a workflow variable `{skf_scripts_path}` resolved on activation — probe `{project-root}/_bmad/skf/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py` first, fall back to `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/skf-atomic-write.py` for dev checkouts, and bind the first hit. Replace every hardcoded `{project-root}/src/shared/scripts/` occurrence in step-07, step-09, and `shared/health-check.md` with `{skf_scripts_path}/`.

1	---
2	nextStepFile: './step-04b-external-validators.md'
3	outputFile: '{forge_version}/test-report-{skill_name}-{run_id}.md'
4	outputFormatsFile: 'assets/output-section-formats.md'
5	scoringRulesFile: 'references/scoring-rules.md'
6	migrationSectionRules: 'references/migration-section-rules.md'
7	---
8	
9	# Step 4: Coherence Check
10	
11	## STEP GOAL:
12	
13	Validate internal consistency of the skill documentation. In contextual mode (stack skills): verify that all cross-references in SKILL.md point to real files, types match their declarations, and integration patterns are complete. In naive mode (individual skills): perform basic structural validation only.
14	
15	## Rules
16	
17	- Use subprocess optimization: grep for references, then per-reference deep validation
18	- For each reference in contextual mode, launch a subprocess to validate the target — do not shortcut
19	- Analysis depth is conditional on testMode (naive vs contextual)
20	
21	## MANDATORY SEQUENCE
22	
23	**CRITICAL:** Follow this sequence exactly. Do not skip, reorder, or improvise unless user explicitly requests a change.
24	
25	### 1. Check Test Mode
26	
27	Read `testMode` from `{outputFile}` frontmatter.
28	
29	**IF naive mode → Execute Naive Coherence (Section 2)**
30	**IF contextual mode → Execute Contextual Coherence (Sections 3-5)**
31	
32	### 2. Naive Mode: Concrete Structural Validation (H1)
33	
34	Perform the following explicit checks (no hand-waving — each recipe is a shell recipe or a literal pattern). Severity assignments are binding; do not relax them.
35	
36	**2.1 Required sections present.** For each required top-level H2, run `grep -n "^## {section}" SKILL.md`:
37	- `## Description` (or frontmatter `description` field — either satisfies)
38	- `## Usage` or `## Examples`
39	- `## Exports` or equivalent API surface heading
40	- **Zero matches for any required section → High severity** finding: `naive-coherence — missing required section: {section}`
41	
42	**2.2 Code fence balance.** Count triple-backtick fences with `grep -c '^```' SKILL.md`. **Odd count → High severity** finding: `naive-coherence — unbalanced code fence (unclosed block)`.
43	
44	**2.3 Language tags on fences.** `grep -n '^```$' SKILL.md` finds bare fences. **Each match → Medium severity** finding: `naive-coherence — code fence at line {N} missing language tag`.
45	
46	**2.4 Exports cross-used in Usage section.** For each function name reported in the step-03 subagent inventory (`exports[].name` where `kind == "function"` or `kind == "method"`):
47	- `grep -c "{export.name}" SKILL.md` restricted to the Usage section (find the `## Usage` anchor from §2.1 and the next `^## ` anchor; count within that span).
48	- **Zero occurrences → High severity** finding: `naive-coherence — exported {kind} \`{name}\` is not referenced in the Usage section`. This catches the "documented but unused" failure mode that trivially fails discovery testing.
49	
50	**2.5 Async/sync consistency.** For every export with `async` in its description prose (grep for `\basync\b` in the description segment), check the corresponding code example segment for `await` / `async` keywords:
51	- Description says async + example shows no `await` → **High severity** finding: `naive-coherence — \`{name}\` described as async but example lacks \`await\``
52	- Description says sync + example uses `await {name}` → **High severity** finding: `naive-coherence — \`{name}\` described as sync but example awaits it`
53	
54	**2.6 Table syntax.** `grep -nE '^\|.*\|$' SKILL.md | head` — for each table row, verify adjacent rows have the same pipe count (split on `|` and compare column count). **Column-count drift → Medium severity** finding: `naive-coherence — table row at line {N} has {X} columns; neighboring rows have {Y}`.
55	
56	**2.7 Scripts & Assets section.** If `{skillDir}/scripts/` or `{skillDir}/assets/` exists, `grep -n '^## Scripts' SKILL.md`:
57	- Directory exists AND no `## Scripts` section → **Medium severity** finding: `naive-coherence — scripts/assets directory exists but Scripts & Assets section missing` (per `{scoringRulesFile}`)
58	
59	**Hard rule:** 0 findings across §§2.1–2.7 = naive coherence PASS. ≥1 finding = rerank per the severity rubric above; the count and severity list are appended to the Coherence Analysis output in §6.
60	
61	Build the findings list:
62	
63	```json
64	{
65	  "structural_issues": [
66	    {"type": "missing_section", "severity": "High", "detail": "No 'Usage' section found", "line": null},
67	    {"type": "unbalanced_fence", "severity": "High", "detail": "3 opening fences, 2 closing", "line": null},
68	    {"type": "export_not_in_usage", "severity": "High", "detail": "exported function `formatDate` never referenced in Usage section", "line": 42},
69	    {"type": "async_mismatch", "severity": "High", "detail": "`fetchData` described async but example lacks await", "line": 67}
70	  ],
71	  "issues_found": 4
72	}
73	```
74	
75	**After naive coherence → Execute Section 2b if gate conditions met, then skip to Section 6 (Append Results)**
76	
77	### 2b. Migration/Deprecation Verification (Mode-Independent)
78	
79	Apply rules from `{migrationSectionRules}`. That file is the single source of
80	truth for the gate, scope, and case rules; §5b below applies the same rules on
81	the contextual path.
82	
83	**After Section 2b (naive path) → Skip to Section 6 (Append Results)**
84	
85	### 3. Contextual Mode: Extract References
86	
87	Scan SKILL.md for all cross-references:
88	
89	**Reference types to extract:**
90	- File path references (`./path/to/file.ts`, `../shared/types.ts`)
91	- Skill references (`See SKILL.md for {other-skill}`, `Integrates with {package}`)
92	- Type imports (`import { Type } from './module'`)
93	- Integration pattern references (middleware chains, plugin hooks, shared state)
94	- Script/asset references (`scripts/{file}`, `assets/{file}`) in SKILL.md body
95	
96	Launch a subprocess to grep/regex SKILL.md for reference patterns and return all found references with line numbers as structured JSON (`references_found[]` with line, type, target fields). If subprocess unavailable, scan in main thread.
97	
98	### 4. Contextual Mode: Validate Each Reference
99	
100	DO NOT BE LAZY — For EACH reference found, launch a subprocess that:
101	
102	1. Checks if the target exists (file exists, skill exists, type is declared)
103	2. If target exists, validates the reference is accurate:
104	   - File path references: file exists at specified path
105	   - Type imports: type is actually exported from the referenced module
106	   - Skill references: referenced skill exists in skills output folder
107	   - Integration patterns: documented pattern matches actual implementation
108	   - Script/asset references: verify the referenced file exists in the skill's `scripts/` or `assets/` directory
109	3. Returns structured validation JSON per reference (reference, line, target_exists, type_match, signature_match, issues[])
110	
111	If subprocess unavailable, validate each reference in main thread.
112	
113	4. **Scripts/assets directory check:** If a `scripts/` or `assets/` directory exists alongside SKILL.md, verify that a "Scripts & Assets" section (Section 7b) is present in SKILL.md. This directory-level check applies in both modes (naive mode performs it in Section 2; contextual mode performs it here alongside per-reference validation). Flag absence as Medium severity gap per `{scoringRulesFile}`.
114	
115	5. **Path containment (S8):** for every resolved reference target, compute its canonical path (`os.path.realpath`) and require that it lives inside `{skillDir}` OR inside `{source_path}` (the extraction tree recorded in metadata.json). References whose canonical path escapes both roots (e.g. `../../../etc/passwd`, absolute paths to unrelated dirs, symlink redirections outside the skill or its source) are **High severity** findings: `coherence — reference escapes skill/source sandbox: {raw_ref} → {canonical_path}`. Do NOT validate the target's contents for escaping references — the escape itself is the finding.
116	
117	### 5. Contextual Mode: Check Integration Pattern Completeness
118	
119	For stack skills, verify integration patterns are complete:
120	
121	- **All documented integration points have corresponding code examples**
122	- **Shared types are consistently used across referenced components**
123	- **Middleware/plugin chains show complete flow, not fragments**
124	- **Event handlers reference valid event types**
125	
126	Build integration completeness findings:
127	
128	```json
129	{
130	  "patterns_documented": 5,
131	  "patterns_complete": 4,
132	  "incomplete_patterns": [
133	    {
134	      "pattern": "Auth middleware chain",
135	      "issue": "Shows middleware registration but not the handler function signature",
136	      "line": 95
137	    }
138	  ]
139	}
140	```
141	
142	**Zero integration patterns:** If no integration patterns are documented in SKILL.md (e.g., a contextual-mode skill that uses shared types but has no middleware chains, plugin hooks, or event flows): record `patterns_documented: 0`, `patterns_complete: 0`. The coherence score will use reference validity alone — see `{scoringRulesFile}` Coherence Score Aggregation: "If no integration patterns exist, combined coherence equals reference validity."
143	
144	### 5b. Migration/Deprecation Verification (Contextual Path)
145	
146	Apply rules from `{migrationSectionRules}`. Same rules as §2b — the reference
147	file is the single source of truth. Append findings to the coherence analysis
148	results.
149	
150	### 5c. Calculate Coherence Scores
151	
152	**Contextual mode only.** Calculate coherence percentages using the formulas defined in `{scoringRulesFile}` — Coherence Score Aggregation section:
153	
154	```
155	reference_validity = (valid_references / total_references) * 100
156	integration_completeness = (complete_patterns / total_patterns) * 100
157	combined_coherence = (reference_validity * 0.6) + (integration_completeness * 0.4)
158	```
159	
160	**Edge case:** If no integration patterns are documented (patterns_documented = 0), combined coherence equals reference validity alone. Do not divide by zero.
161	
162	These values fill the `{percentage}%` placeholders in the output template loaded in Section 6.
163	
164	### 6. Append Coherence Analysis to Output
165	
166	Load `{outputFormatsFile}` and use the appropriate Coherence Analysis section format (naive or contextual) to append findings to `{outputFile}`.
167	
168	### 7. Report Coherence Results
169	
170	**For Naive Mode:**
171	"**Coherence check complete (naive mode).**
172	
173	Basic structural validation of **{skill_name}**:
174	- {N} structural issues found
175	- Coherence category not scored (weight redistributed to coverage)
176	
177	**Proceeding to external validation...**"
178	
179	**For Contextual Mode:**
180	"**Coherence check complete (contextual mode).**
181	
182	Reference validation of **{skill_name}**:
183	- References: {valid}/{total} valid ({percentage}%)
184	- Integration patterns: {complete}/{total} complete ({percentage}%)
185	- Combined coherence: {percentage}%
186	
187	**{N} issues found** — details in Coherence Analysis section.
188	
189	**Proceeding to external validation...**"
190	
191	### 8. Auto-Proceed
192	
193	Display: "**Proceeding to external validation...**"
194	
195	#### Menu Handling Logic:
196	
197	- After coherence analysis is complete, update {outputFile} frontmatter stepsCompleted, then immediately load, read entire file, then execute {nextStepFile}
198	
199	#### EXECUTION RULES:
200	
201	- This is an auto-proceed validation step with no user choices
202	- Proceed directly to next step after coherence is analyzed
203	
204	## CRITICAL STEP COMPLETION NOTE
205	
206	ONLY WHEN coherence analysis is complete (naive structural or contextual full validation), the Coherence Analysis section has been appended to {outputFile}, and coherence scores (if contextual) have been calculated, will you then load and read fully `{nextStepFile}` to execute external validation.
207	
208	
```

{

  "old_string": "**2.3 Language tags on fences.** `grep -n '^```$' SKILL.md` finds bare fences. **Each match → Medium severity** finding: `naive-coherence — code fence at line {N} missing language tag`.",
  "new_string": "**2.3 Language tags on opening fences.** Only **opening** fences are required to carry a language tag; closing fences are bare by markdown convention and must NOT be flagged. Do not use a plain `grep -n '^```$' SKILL.md` — that flags every closing fence and produces one false positive per well-formed code block.\n\nUse a stateful open/close scan (toggle `in_code` on each `^```` line; flag only the line where `in_code` transitions 0→1 with no trailing language tag):\n\n```python\nin_code = False\nfor i, line in enumerate(open('SKILL.md'), 1):\n    s = line.rstrip('\\n')\n    if s.startswith('```'):\n        if not in_code:\n            if s == '```':\n                print(f'{i}: bare opening fence')\n            in_code = True\n        else:\n            in_code = False\n```\n\n**Each flagged opening fence → Medium severity** finding: `naive-coherence — opening code fence at line {N} missing language tag`."
}
```

{
  "replace_all": false,
  "file_path": "/home/armel/Projects/OSS/bmad-module-skill-forge/src/skf-test-skill/steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md",
  "old_string": "**2.6 Table syntax.** `grep -nE '^\\|.*\\|$' SKILL.md | head` — for each table row, verify adjacent rows have the same pipe count (split on `|` and compare column count). **Column-count drift → Medium severity** finding: `naive-coherence — table row at line {N} has {X} columns; neighboring rows have {Y}`.",
  "new_string": "**2.6 Table syntax.** `grep -nE '^\\|.*\\|$' SKILL.md | head` — for each table row, normalize escaped pipes (`\\|`) before splitting, then verify adjacent rows have the same column count. **Escaped pipes appear inside TypeScript union types and discriminated payloads** (e.g. `string \\| undefined`) and must not inflate the count.\n\nRecipe:\n\n```bash\n# Normalize `\\|` to a placeholder, split on |, count, restore.\ngrep -nE '^\\|.*\\|$' SKILL.md \\\n  | sed 's/\\\\|/\\x00/g' \\\n  | awk -F'|' '{print NR, NF-2}'   # -2 drops the empty leading/trailing fields\n```\n\nOr equivalent: hand off to a proper markdown-table parser. A plain `split on |` WILL produce false \"column drift\" findings on any table whose cells contain union types.\n\n**Column-count drift → Medium severity** finding: `naive-coherence — table row at line {N} has {X} columns; neighboring rows have {Y}`."
}
```

40	2. **Source Access Resolution** — 5-state waterfall to determine how source files will be read and sets `analysis_confidence`
41	
42	### 1. Extract Documented Exports from SKILL.md
43	
44	<!-- Subagent delegation: read SKILL.md + references/*.md, return compact JSON inventory -->
45	
46	Delegate reading of the skill under test to a subagent. The subagent receives the path to SKILL.md (and the `references/` directory path if it exists) and MUST:
47	1. Read SKILL.md
48	2. If a `references/` directory exists alongside SKILL.md and SKILL.md's `## Full` headings are absent or stubs, also read all `references/*.md` files
49	3. ONLY return this compact JSON inventory — no prose, no extra commentary:
50	
51	```json
52	{
53	  "exports": [
54	    {"name": "functionName", "kind": "function", "params": "...", "return_type": "...", "description": "..."},
55	    {"name": "ClassName", "kind": "class", "methods": ["..."], "properties": ["..."]},
56	    {"name": "TypeName", "kind": "type", "fields": ["..."]},
57	    {"name": "CONST_NAME", "kind": "constant", "values": ["..."]},
58	    {"name": "useHook", "kind": "hook", "usage_signature": "..."}
59	  ],
60	  "capabilities": ["brief capability descriptions from the skill overview"],
61	  "references": ["references/api-reference.md", "references/type-definitions.md"],
62	  "cross_check_mismatches": [
63	    {
64	      "export": "functionName",
65	      "skill_md_line": 42,
66	      "reference_file": "references/api-reference.md",
67	      "reference_line": 18,
68	      "issue": "description of the signature mismatch"
69	    }
70	  ]
71	}
72	```
73	
74	**Parent uses this JSON summary as the documented inventory.** Do not load SKILL.md or references file contents into parent context.
75	
76	#### 1a. Parent-Side Schema Validation + Spot-Check (MANDATORY)
77	
78	test-skill is a quality gate — it MUST NOT trust subagent output blindly. Before any downstream step consumes the inventory, the parent performs two checks and HALTs on any failure:
79	
80	**Schema validation (required keys + types):**
81	
82	1. Parse the subagent response as JSON. On parse failure → HALT "coverage-check: subagent response not valid JSON".
83	2. Required keys present: `skill_name` (string), `exports` (list), `cross_check_mismatches` (list — may be empty). Missing key or wrong type → HALT "coverage-check: subagent JSON schema invalid — missing/typo: {key}".
84	3. Each `exports[]` entry must be a dict with at minimum `name` (non-empty string) and `kind` (one of `function|class|type|constant|hook|interface|method`). Reject entries violating this; if >0 rejections, HALT "coverage-check: subagent returned malformed export entries — {count} entries do not match schema".
85	4. `cross_check_mismatches[]` entries (when non-empty) must carry `export`, `skill_md_line`, `reference_file`, `reference_line`, `issue`. Missing fields → HALT.
86	
87	**Spot-check (ground-truth verification, zero-hallucination guard):**
88	
89	1. If `len(exports) == 0`: skip the spot-check (no names to verify). Zero-exports policy is handled in section 3 (B1 zero-exports guard).
90	2. Otherwise, sample `min(3, len(exports))` exports deterministically — by default take indices `[0, len//2, len-1]` (first, middle, last) from the `exports` array after a stable sort by `name`.
91	3. For each sampled export, run: `grep -n "{export.name}" {resolved_skill_package}/SKILL.md` in the parent context. The name MUST appear at least once.
92	4. If ANY sampled name returns zero matches, HALT "coverage-check: subagent inventory failed ground-truth spot-check — `{name}` claimed as export but absent from SKILL.md".
93	
94	These checks catch two hallucination classes: schema-shape drift (subagent paraphrased or dropped the contract) and fabricated exports (subagent invented names not in the document). Both are disqualifying for a grader skill — do not downgrade to a warning.
95	
96	**Split-body traversal** is handled inside the subagent: if `references/` exists and `## Full` headings are absent or stubs in SKILL.md, the subagent extends its scan to all `references/*.md` files and includes them in the `exports` array. After split-body, Tier 2 content (Full API Reference, Full Type Definitions) lives in reference files — the inventory must reflect the full skill content regardless of where it resides.
97	
98	### 1b. Cross-Check Split-Body Consistency
99

80	
81	**Note:** `skill-check` may return a non-zero exit code even when `errorCount` is 0. Always rely on the parsed JSON output, not the shell exit code.
82	
83	Store in context: `skill_check_score`, `skill_check_diagnostics`
84	
85	**If skill-check fails entirely:** Record `skill_check_score: N/A`, log warning, continue.
86	
87	### 3. Run tessl
88	
89	**Check availability (short probe — 15s timeout):**
90	
91	```bash
92	timeout 15s npx --no-install -y tessl --version 2>/dev/null
93	```
94	
95	Same rationale as the skill-check probe above: `--no-install` + `timeout 15s`
96	prevent a cold-cache fetch from stalling the workflow. If the probe exits
97	non-zero OR the 15s timeout trips (exit code `124`), record
98	`tessl_score: N/A` and skip to section 4.
99	
100	**Run review (S2 — 120s timeout; S3 — pinned version):**
101	
102	```bash
103	# S3: pin tessl to a known-working version. Bump TESSL_PIN here (and only
104	# here) when upgrading. Treat regex parse failure below as `tessl_score: N/A`.
105	TESSL_PIN="@anthropic/tessl@0.4"
106	timeout 120s npx -y "$TESSL_PIN" skill review {skillDir}
107	```
108	
109	Timeout handling mirrors skill-check: exit `124` → `tessl_score: N/A` with reason `timeout-120s`. If the percentage regex (`/(Description|Content|Review Score):\s*(\d+)%/`) returns fewer than three matches, record `tessl_score: N/A` with reason `parse-failure` and include the first 200 chars of output in evidence-report for debugging.
110	
111	**Parse the output** to extract:
112	- `description_score` — percentage (e.g., 100%)
113	- `content_score` — percentage (e.g., 45%)
114	- `review_score` — percentage (e.g., 73%)
115	- `validation_result` — PASSED/FAILED
116	- `judge_suggestions[]` — list of improvement suggestions
117	
118	The tessl output is human-readable text, not JSON. Parse the percentage values from lines like "Description: 100%", "Content: 45%", "Review Score: 73%".
119	
120	Store in context: `tessl_description_score`, `tessl_content_score`, `tessl_review_score`, `tessl_suggestions`
121	
122	**If tessl content score < 70%:** Flag a warning:
123	
124	"**Content quality warning:** tessl scored content at {score}%. This often indicates SKILL.md lacks inline actionable content (e.g., after split-body). If this is a split-body skill, the score drop is expected — tessl evaluates only SKILL.md body, not `references/*.md` (see scoring-rules.md). Consider using selective split to keep actionable content inline."
125	
126	**If tessl fails entirely:** Record `tessl_score: N/A`, log warning, continue.
127	
128	### 4. Calculate Combined External Score
129	
130	**If both tools ran:**
131	
132	```
133	external_score = (skill_check_score + tessl_review_score) / 2
134	```
135	
136	Note: `skill_check_score` is 0-100, `tessl_review_score` is 0-100%. Both are on the same scale.
137	
138	**If only one tool ran:** Use that tool's score as the external score.
139	
140	**If neither tool ran:** Record `external_score: N/A`. The scoring step will redistribute the external validation weight.
141	
142	### 5. Append External Validation to Output
143	
144	Append to `{outputFile}`:
145	
146	```markdown
147	## External Validation
148	
149	### skill-check
```

If the percentage regex (`/(Description|Content|Review Score):\\s*(\\d+)%/`) returns fewer than three matches, record `tessl_score: N/A` with reason `parse-failure` and include the first 200 chars of output in evidence-report for debugging.\n\n**Registry-404 branch:** if the invocation emits `npm error 404 Not Found` or the npx wrapper exits with a not-found condition, record `tessl_score: N/A` with reason `pin-not-on-registry` and continue. This branch exists because tessl has historically shipped under shifting scope/tag combinations; do not HALT the workflow on a missing registry entry."
}
```

```
1	# Scoring Rules
2	
3	## Default Threshold
4	
5	**Pass threshold:** 80%
6	
7	## Category Weights
8	
9	| Category               | Weight | Description                                                                               |
10	|------------------------|--------|-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
11	| Export Coverage        | 36%    | Percentage of source exports documented in SKILL.md                                       |
12	| Signature Accuracy     | 22%    | Documented signatures match actual source signatures                                      |
13	| Type Coverage          | 14%    | Types and interfaces referenced are complete                                              |
14	| Coherence (contextual) | 18%    | Cross-references valid, integration patterns complete                                     |
15	| Coherence (naive)      | 0%     | Not applicable — weight redistributed to other categories                                 |
16	| External Validation    | 10%    | Average of skill-check quality score + tessl average score (redistributed if unavailable) |
17	
18	## Naive Mode Weight Redistribution
19	
20	The following weights replace the default table for naive mode. The 18% coherence weight from the default table has been proportionally redistributed into these values. Do not re-redistribute for coherence (already handled in this table). Quick-tier redistribution (zeroing Signature Accuracy and Type Coverage) still applies as an additional step.
21	
22	When running in naive mode (no coherence category):
23	- Export Coverage: 45%
24	- Signature Accuracy: 25%
25	- Type Coverage: 20%
26	- External Validation: 10%
27	
28	## External Validation Unavailable
29	
30	When neither skill-check nor tessl is available, redistribute the 10% external validation weight proportionally to the other active categories. When only one tool is available, use that tool's score as the external validation score.
31	
32	## tessl and Split-Body Interaction
33	
34	tessl evaluates SKILL.md body content only — it does not read `references/*.md` files. After split-body extraction, the tessl content score will drop significantly (e.g., 65% to 38%) because Tier 2 content is no longer inline. This is expected behavior and does not reflect actual content quality. When reporting scores for a split-body skill, note: "tessl content score reflects post-split inline content only. Use the pre-split tessl score as the content quality baseline."
35	
36	## Tier-Dependent Scoring
37	
38	### Quick Tier (no tools)
39	- Export Coverage: file/structure existence check only
40	- Signature Accuracy: skipped (no AST)
41	- Type Coverage: skipped (no AST)
42	- Score based on: structural completeness only
43	- Weight redistribution: skipped categories' weights (Signature Accuracy 22% + Type Coverage 14%) redistributed proportionally to remaining active categories
44	
45	### Docs-Only Mode (all [EXT:...] citations, any tier)
46	
47	When `docs_only_mode: true` is set by step-03 (indicating a skill where all SKILL.md citations are `[EXT:...]` format with no local source code):
48	
49	- **Signature Accuracy:** Not scored (no source to compare against)
50	- **Type Coverage:** Not scored (no source to compare against)
51	- **Weight redistribution:** Same as Quick tier — Signature Accuracy (22%) and Type Coverage (14%) weights redistributed proportionally to remaining active categories
52	- **Export Coverage basis:** Documentation completeness rather than source coverage. Score = (documented_items_with_complete_descriptions / total_documented_items) * 100. A "complete" item has: description, parameters (if function/method), and return type (if function/method).
53	- **Coherence:** Standard rules for the detected mode (naive or contextual) apply unchanged
54	
55	This is functionally identical to Quick tier weight redistribution but with a different coverage denominator (self-consistency instead of source comparison).
56	
57	**S4 external-validator requirement for docs-only:** docs-only mode removes two categories (Signature Accuracy, Type Coverage) from scoring. If External Validation is ALSO unavailable, the evidence base collapses to Coverage alone (naive) or Coverage + Coherence (contextual) — which in the naive/Quick case trips the minimum-evidence floor (INCONCLUSIVE). To keep docs-only skills gradable when external validators are present but still deterministic when they are missing: **when `docsOnly: true` AND `externalValidation is null`, step-05 MUST cap `totalScore` at `threshold - 1` (forcing FAIL) before the INCONCLUSIVE floor is evaluated.** This prevents a docs-only skill from PASSing with only one or two redistributed categories carrying all the weight. Implement in step-05 §4 as a pre-compare cap, recorded in the report as `scoring_notes: docs-only without external validators — capped below threshold per S4`.
58	
59	### State 2 Source Access (Any Tier, Provenance-Map Only)
60	
61	When source is not locally available and analysis resolves to State 2 (provenance-map baseline per source-access-protocol.md):
62	
63	- **Signature Accuracy:** N/A — provenance-map stores parameters as flat string arrays; verification is string comparison only, not semantic AST verification. Type aliases (`str` vs `String`, `list` vs `List[Any]`) cannot be resolved without live source.
64	- **Type Coverage:** N/A — cannot verify type completeness without local source access for AST re-parsing.
65	- **Weight redistribution:** Same as Quick tier — Signature Accuracy (22%) and Type Coverage (14%) weights redistributed proportionally to remaining active categories (Export Coverage, Coherence, External Validation).
66	- **Applies regardless of detected tier** (including Forge, Forge+, Deep) whenever `analysis_confidence` is `provenance-map` and local source is unavailable.
67	- **Export Coverage denominator:** Uses the union of provenance-map entry names and metadata.json `exports[]` names (per source-access-protocol.md State 2 rules).
68	
69	Note: When provenance-map entries are predominantly T1 (AST-verified at compilation time), the coverage and name-matching data is already at highest confidence. The N/A categories reflect the inability to re-verify at test time, not low-quality extraction data.
70	
71	**S9 State 2 undercount risk acknowledgement:** provenance-map is a cached extraction snapshot — if the source has evolved since extraction, public API adds/removes will NOT surface in Export Coverage (denominator is frozen to the provenance-map union). When `state2: true` AND step-03 records any provenance vs metadata divergence (e.g. union > either source by >5%), apply a flat **10% deduction** to `exportCoverage` before calling the scoring script, AND set `analysis_confidence: provenance-map` (already set) with a report note: `scoring_notes: State 2 undercount risk acknowledged — 10% deduction applied to Export Coverage`. Rationale: the skill cannot be reliably scored on a frozen denominator when the cache is known to disagree with its own metadata; prefer understating over overstating.
72	
73	### Forge Tier (ast-grep)
74	- Export Coverage: AST-backed export comparison
75	- Signature Accuracy: AST-verified signature matching
76	- Type Coverage: AST-verified type completeness
77	- Full scoring formula applied
78	
79	### Forge+ Tier (ast-grep + ccc)
80	- Same scoring as Forge tier — ccc provides pre-ranking but does not change scoring weights
81	- Improved extraction coverage (from ccc pre-discovery) may increase T1 count, but scoring formula is identical to Forge
82	- Full scoring formula applied
83	
84	### Deep Tier (ast-grep + gh + QMD)
85	- All Forge tier checks plus:
86	- Cross-repository reference verification
87	- QMD knowledge enrichment for coherence
88	- Full scoring formula with maximum depth
89	- **Migration & Deprecation Warnings section:** If T2-future annotations exist in the enrichment data, verify that Section 4b is present in SKILL.md Tier 1 and that each warning traces to a T2 provenance citation. If no T2-future annotations exist, Section 4b should normally be absent (not empty). Presence/absence mismatch is a Medium severity gap — with one Info-severity exception for historical-migration content (completed package renames, consolidated import paths, shipped API cutovers that remain load-bearing for training-data drift remediation). See `steps-c/step-04-coherence-check.md` §2b/§5b for the three-case rule.
90	
91	## Redistribution Combinations Matrix (M3 — terminology + determinism)
92	
93	The table below enumerates every `(mode × tier × docsOnly × state2)` cell and
94	the redistribution outcome. Two cells map to the same **equivalence class**
95	whenever their skip set and base-weight table are identical — the scoring
96	script then emits the same `weights` / `activeCategories` / `skippedCategories`
97	output regardless of which specific cell produced it. The rightmost column
98	cites the representative fixture in
99	`test/fixtures/compute-score-contract.json` used to pin the class's math.
100	
101	| # | mode       | tier     | docsOnly | state2 | Base table     | Skipped (sig/type)    | Skipped (ext if null) | Equiv. class | Representative fixture        |
102	|---|------------|----------|----------|--------|----------------|-----------------------|-----------------------|--------------|-------------------------------|
103	| 1 | contextual | Deep     | F        | F      | contextual     | —                     | if null               | **A**        | `suite_a_all_active`          |
104	| 2 | contextual | Forge+   | F        | F      | contextual     | —                     | if null               | A            | `suite_k_forge_plus`          |
105	| 3 | contextual | Forge    | F        | F      | contextual     | —                     | if null               | A            | `suite_p_contextual_forge`    |
106	| 4 | contextual | Quick    | F        | F      | contextual     | Quick tier            | if null               | **B**        | `suite_c_quick_tier`          |
107	| 5 | contextual | Deep     | T        | F      | contextual     | docs-only             | if null               | B            | `suite_r_contextual_deep_docsonly` |
108	| 6 | contextual | Forge+   | T        | F      | contextual     | docs-only             | if null               | B            | (equiv. — see `suite_r_*`)    |
109	| 7 | contextual | Forge    | T        | F      | contextual     | docs-only             | if null               | B            | (equiv.)                      |
110	| 8 | contextual | Quick    | T        | F      | contextual     | Quick tier + docs-only| if null               | B            | `suite_f_docs_only`           |
111	| 9 | contextual | Deep     | F        | T      | contextual     | State 2               | if null               | B            | `suite_g_state2`              |
112	| 10| contextual | Forge+   | F        | T      | contextual     | State 2               | if null               | B            | (equiv.)                      |
113	| 11| contextual | Forge    | F        | T      | contextual     | State 2               | if null               | B            | (equiv.)                      |
114	| 12| contextual | Quick    | F        | T      | contextual     | Quick + State 2       | if null               | B            | (equiv. — Quick+state2)       |
115	| 13| contextual | *        | T        | T      | contextual     | docs-only + State 2   | if null               | B            | (equiv.)                      |
116	| 14| naive      | Deep     | F        | F      | naive          | —                     | if null               | **C**        | `suite_q_naive_deep`          |
117	| 15| naive      | Forge+   | F        | F      | naive          | —                     | if null               | C            | (equiv.)                      |
118	| 16| naive      | Forge    | F        | F      | naive          | —                     | if null               | C            | `suite_b_naive`               |
119	| 17| naive      | Quick    | F        | F      | naive          | Quick tier            | if null               | **D**        | `suite_e_triple_skip`         |
120	| 18| naive      | Deep     | T        | F      | naive          | docs-only             | if null               | D            | `suite_s_naive_deep_docsonly` |
121	| 19| naive      | Forge+   | T        | F      | naive          | docs-only             | if null               | D            | (equiv.)                      |
122	| 20| naive      | Forge    | T        | F      | naive          | docs-only             | if null               | D            | (equiv.)                      |
123	| 21| naive      | Quick    | T        | F      | naive          | Quick + docs-only     | if null               | D            | `suite_o_input_echo`          |
124	| 22| naive      | Deep     | F        | T      | naive          | State 2               | if null               | D            | `suite_t_naive_state2`        |
125	| 23| naive      | Forge+   | F        | T      | naive          | State 2               | if null               | D            | (equiv.)                      |
126	| 24| naive      | Forge    | F        | T      | naive          | State 2               | if null               | D            | (equiv.)                      |
127	| 25| naive      | Quick    | F        | T      | naive          | Quick + State 2       | if null               | D            | (equiv.)                      |
128	| 26| naive      | *        | T        | T      | naive          | docs-only + State 2   | if null               | D            | (equiv.)                      |
129	
130	**How to read the matrix.** The four equivalence classes A/B/C/D are
131	exhaustive: the scoring script's weight-redistribution output depends ONLY on
132	(base table, sig/type skip, ext skip), so every row mapping to class X emits
133	the same final weights for identical input scores. "(equiv.)" rows are *not*
134	missing coverage — they reduce algebraically to a listed representative. The
135	M3 fixture set ships at least one representative per equivalence class and one
136	representative per unique skip-reason combination so the `skipReasons` string
137	is also pinned (e.g. "Quick tier", "docs-only mode", "State 2
138	(provenance-map)", "Quick tier + docs-only mode").
139	
140	**Quick-tier INCONCLUSIVE interaction.** Rows 4, 8, 12, 17, 21, 25 all force
141	the minimum-evidence floor to evaluate "Quick tier + Export Coverage alone"
142	cases separately — see `Result Determination` below. The matrix above is the
143	pre-floor redistribution only; INCONCLUSIVE verdict determination runs after.
144	
145	## Score Calculation
146	
147	```
148	score = sum(category_weight * category_score) for each category
149	category_score = (items_passing / items_total) * 100
150	```
151	
152	## Coherence Score Aggregation (Contextual Mode)
153	
154	```
155	reference_validity = (valid_references / total_references) * 100
156	integration_completeness = (complete_patterns / total_patterns) * 100
157	combined_coherence = (reference_validity * 0.6) + (integration_completeness * 0.4)
158	```
159	
160	If no integration patterns exist, combined coherence equals reference validity.
161	
162	## Result Determination
163	
164	Three-state gate — **PASS / FAIL / INCONCLUSIVE**. `INCONCLUSIVE` is not PASS and not FAIL; it signals insufficient evidence to grade the skill. Downstream workflows MUST treat `INCONCLUSIVE` as a hard gate — do not export, do not auto-retry, surface to the human.
165	
166	- **Minimum-Evidence Floor (MANDATORY — applies before PASS/FAIL comparison):**
167	  - `active_categories` = count of categories with a non-zero final weight *after* all redistribution (Quick tier, docs-only, State 2, external-validator-unavailable). Categories with a redistributed weight of 0 do not count as active, even if they received a score.
168	  - **If `active_categories < 2`** → force `result: INCONCLUSIVE` with rationale `"insufficient evidence: only {N} active category"`. A single active category cannot cross-validate itself and a PASS would be a false signal.
169	  - **If `tier == "Quick"` AND the sole active contributor is Export Coverage** → force `result: INCONCLUSIVE` with rationale `"Quick tier: Export Coverage alone is insufficient evidence — add a second active category by upgrading tier or enabling external validators"`. This catches the degenerate case where every signature/type/coherence/external category gets redistributed to 0 and Export Coverage is doing all the work.
170	  - The floor is enforced by `scripts/compute-score.py`. The step-05 scoring step reads `result` from the script output and writes it into the test report frontmatter unchanged.
171	
172	- Otherwise:
173	  - score >= threshold → PASS
174	  - score < threshold → FAIL
175	
176	The floor is intentionally conservative: skf-test-skill grades other skills, so a false PASS has catastrophic downstream effects (polluted exports, misleading feasibility data). Falling back to INCONCLUSIVE is always preferred over a low-evidence PASS.
177	
178	## Gap Severity
179	
180	| Severity | Criteria                                                                                                       |
181	|----------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
182	| Critical | Missing exported function/class documentation                                                                  |
183	| High     | Signature mismatch between source and SKILL.md                                                                 |
184	| Medium   | Missing type or interface documentation                                                                        |
185	| Medium   | Migration section present/absent mismatch with T2-future annotation data (Deep tier)                           |
186	| Medium   | Metadata drift — intra-cluster export counts diverge (barrel: `stats.exports_public_api` vs `exports[].length`; or documented-surface: `stats.exports_documented` vs provenance-map entry count; >10% divergence) |
187	| Medium   | Denominator inflation — stratified-scope `scope.include` union exceeds provenance-map entry count by >25% (brief missing `scope.tier_a_include`) |
188	| Medium   | Script/asset directory exists but no Scripts & Assets section in SKILL.md                                      |
189	| Medium   | Scripts & Assets section references file not found in scripts/ or assets/ directory                            |
190	| Low      | Script/asset file present without provenance entry in provenance-map.json file_entries                         |
191	| Low      | Missing optional metadata or examples                                                                          |
192	| Low      | Description trigger optimization recommended (third-person voice, negative triggers, or keyword coverage gaps) |
193	| Info     | Style suggestions, non-blocking observations                                                                   |
194	| Info     | Discovery testing not performed — realistic prompt testing recommended before export                           |
195	| Info     | Multi-denominator reporting — barrel vs documented-surface clusters diverge by design (>10% cross-cluster)     |
196	
```
